  SA 6967. Mr. MARKEY submitted an amendment intended to be proposed to 
amendment SA 6883 proposed by Mr. Thune (for Mrs. Capito (for herself, 
Mr. Lee, Mr. Whitehouse, and Mr. Heinrich)) to the bill H.R. 2347, to 
amend the Internal Revenue Code of 1986 to exclude from gross income 
any damages, other than punitive damages, received on account of any 
sexual acts or sexual contact; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

        At the end of section 2117, add the following:
       (g) Environmental Impacts.--
       (1) Definitions.--In this subsection:
       (A) Administrator.--The term ``Administrator'' means the 
     Administrator of the Environmental Protection Agency.
       (B) Data center.--The term ``data center'' means any 
     facility, or group of facilities with the same owner in the 
     same utility area that--
       (i) primarily contains electronic equipment used to host 
     information and information systems accessed by other systems 
     or by users on other devices both in and outside of the State 
     in which the facility or group of facilities is located;
       (ii) is a free-standing structure or a facility within a 
     larger structure that uses environmental control equipment to 
     maintain the proper conditions for the operation of 
     electronic equipment;
       (iii) has an energy demand greater than 50 megawatts; and
       (iv) meets such other criteria as the Federal 
     Communications Commission determines to be appropriate for 
     purposes of this section, including anticircumvention 
     provisions.
       (C) Data center corporation.--The term ``data center 
     corporation'' means any corporation, joint-stock company, 
     partnership, association, business trust, or organized group 
     of persons, whether incorporated or not, or a receiver or 
     trustee of such, that owns or proposes to own a data center.
       (2) Certificate requirements for data centers.--
       (A) Definition.--In this paragraph, the term ``Commission'' 
     means the Federal Communications Commission.
       (B) Certificate required.--
       (i) In general.--Before proposed construction activities 
     may begin with respect to a data center, the data center 
     corporation with respect to the data center shall be required 
     to obtain from the Commission a certificate certifying that 
     the construction of the data center will not harm the public 
     interest.
       (ii) Evaluation of application.--

       (I) In general.--With respect to an application for a 
     certificate described in clause (i), the Commission shall 
     evaluate the potential impacts of the applicable data center 
     on--

       (aa) air quality, including local, upstream, and downstream 
     emissions of air pollutants;
       (bb) water quality, including toxic and priority pollutant 
     concentration levels, temperature, turbidity, and nutrient 
     levels;
       (cc) the local and watershed-level availability and cost of 
     water, including the impacts on wastewater services;
       (dd) the average levels of noise and light within 2 miles 
     of the proposed site of the data center;
       (ee) energy costs and electricity system reliability, 
     particularly for residential and commercial ratepayers within 
     the same regional electricity market as the data center, as 
     determined by the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission;
       (ff) the local ecosystem and wildlife;
       (gg) the local economy, including through the offering of 
     public subsidies and tax exemptions;
       (hh) community well-being, including the establishment of 
     community benefits agreements; and
       (ii) jobs, particularly the creation of full-time jobs with 
     wages that are not less than the prevailing wage rates for 
     the class in the applicable locality.

       (II) Consultation.--In conducting an evaluation required 
     under subclause (I), the Commission shall consult with all 
     relevant Federal, State, local, Tribal, and regional 
     authorities (as determined based on the location of the 
     applicable data center), including--

       (aa) the Environmental Protection Agency, the Department of 
     Energy, the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission, and other 
     Federal agencies determined appropriate by the Commission;
       (bb) local, State, and Tribal--
       (AA) environmental protection agencies;
       (BB) zoning boards;
       (CC) siting and permitting authorities;
       (DD) energy and economic development agencies; and

[[Page S5651]]

       (EE) consumer advocates;
       (cc) State public utility commissions;
       (dd) Transmission Organizations, as defined in section 3 of 
     the Federal Power Act (16 U.S.C. 796);
       (ee) the regional commission or committee that represents 
     the interests of States with respect to the relevant regional 
     electricity market; and
       (ff) other relevant Federal, State, local, Tribal, and 
     regional authorities, as determined appropriate by the 
     Commission.
       (iii) Determination.--The Commission shall issue a 
     certificate described in clause (i) if, after conducting the 
     evaluation required under clause (ii), the Commission 
     determines that the proposed data center will not harm the 
     public interest.
       (iv) Ongoing compliance; enforcement.--After issuing a 
     certificate under clause (i) with respect to a data center, 
     the Commission--

       (I) shall, not later than 2 years after issuing that 
     certificate, and once every 2 years thereafter--

       (aa) assess whether the data center remains in compliance 
     with that certificate, as determined by evaluating the data 
     center pursuant to the requirements under clause (ii); and
       (bb) as part of the assessment required under item (aa), 
     establish a publicly noticed and accessible process through 
     which affected members of the public may submit documentation 
     or provide comment with respect to the certificate; and

       (II) if the Commission, during an assessment conducted 
     under subclause (I) of this clause, determines that a data 
     center does not remain in compliance with that certificate, 
     as determined as described in subclause (I)(aa) of this 
     clause, may take action and pursue remedies to ensure that 
     the data center remains in compliance, including by imposing 
     fines that are proportionate to the duration and degree by 
     which the data center is not in compliance.

       (C) Savings clause.--Nothing in this paragraph shall 
     preempt or render void any State or local regulation relating 
     to the construction or operation of a data center.
       (3) Identifying air pollution and disparate impacts of data 
     center development and investing in community response.--
       (A) In general.--The Administrator may--
       (i) pursuant to subsections (b) through (e) of section 103 
     of the Clean Air Act (42 U.S.C. 7403), make grants, enter 
     into contracts, or carry out other activities described in 
     that section to identify, monitor, and increase community 
     response capabilities against environmental or public health 
     harms from changes in air quality associated with the 
     construction and operation of data centers and associated 
     energy infrastructure, with a priority for activities that 
     serve communities with greater cumulative environmental or 
     public health burdens; and
       (ii) make grants and carry out other activities with 
     respect to the changes in air quality described in paragraph 
     (1) authorized under section 105 of the Clean Air Act (42 
     U.S.C. 7405).
       (B) Authorization of appropriations.--
       (i) In general.--In addition to any other amounts 
     authorized to be appropriated to carry out sections 103 and 
     105 of the Clean Air Act (42 U.S.C. 7403, 7405), there are 
     authorized to be appropriated to the Administrator for each 
     of fiscal years 2027 through 2031, to remain available until 
     expended--

       (I) $50,000,000 to carry out subparagraph (A)(i); and
       (II) $50,000,000 to carry out subparagraph (A)(ii).

       (ii) Administrative expenses.--Of the amounts made 
     available under subclauses (I) and (II) of clause (i) in a 
     fiscal year, the Administrator shall reserve 5 percent for 
     the administrative costs necessary to carry out the 
     activities funded under those subclauses.
       (4) Water quality monitoring.--
       (A) In general.--The Administrator may--
       (i) in addition to the purposes for which grants may be 
     provided under section 106 of the Federal Water Pollution 
     Control Act (33 U.S.C. 1256), make additional grants to, or 
     enter into cooperative agreements or contracts with, entities 
     eligible to receive grants under that section for water 
     quality monitoring and water pollution control related to 
     existing or expected impacts on water systems occurring as a 
     result of the construction and operation of data centers and 
     associated energy infrastructure; and
       (ii) make grants to the entities described in clause (i) to 
     obtain or contract for technical assistance relating to the 
     purposes described in that clause.
       (B) Authorization of appropriations.--In addition to any 
     other amounts authorized to be appropriated to carry out 
     section 106 of the Federal Water Pollution Control Act (33 
     U.S.C. 1256), there are authorized to be appropriated to the 
     Administrator for each of fiscal years 2027 through 2031, to 
     remain available until expended--
       (i) $100,000,000 to carry out subparagraph (A)(i); and
       (ii) $30,000,000 to carry out subparagraph (A)(ii).
       (5) Federal resources for mitigation of noise pollution.--
       (A) Study.--
       (i) In general.--The Administrator shall carry out a study 
     on the health impacts of noise from data centers and on-site 
     energy generation, including an examination of--

       (I) the selection of noise measurement methodologies by 
     relevant State and local agencies with respect to that noise;
       (II) the threshold at which health impacts from that noise 
     is felt;
       (III) the effectiveness of various noise abatement 
     methodologies used at data centers and on-site energy 
     generators; and
       (IV) new and emerging noise abatement methodologies that 
     may, or have the potential to, mitigate the extent of noise 
     pollution impacts from data centers and on-site energy 
     generation.

       (ii) Report.--

       (I) In general.--Not later than 2 years after the date of 
     enactment of this Act, the Administrator shall submit to 
     Congress a report describing the results of the study 
     conducted under clause (i).
       (II) Requirements.--The report required under subclause (I) 
     shall include specific recommendations on new measures that 
     can be implemented to mitigate the impact of data centers and 
     on-site energy generation on surrounding communities.

       (B) Grants under the quiet communities program.--Section 14 
     of the Noise Control Act of 1972 (42 U.S.C. 4913) is 
     amended--
       (i) in subsection (b)--

       (I) in the matter preceding paragraph (1), by striking 
     ``but not limited to'';
       (II) in paragraph (2), by striking ``sections 6, 7, and 8 
     of this Act'' and inserting ``section 6 or 8, or section 
     44715 of title 49, United States Code''; and
       (III) by redesignating paragraphs (1) through (5) as 
     subparagraphs (A) through (E), respectively, and indenting 
     appropriately;

       (ii) in subsection (c)--

       (I) in the matter preceding paragraph (1), by striking 
     ``but not be limited to'' and inserting ``in accordance with 
     Federal authority pursuant to this Act to regulate sources of 
     noise in interstate commerce'';
       (II) in paragraph (1)--

       (aa) in subparagraph (C), by striking ``and'' at the end;
       (bb) by redesignating subparagraphs (A) through (D) as 
     clauses (i) through (iv), respectively, and indenting 
     appropriately; and
       (cc) by adding at the end the following:
       ``(v) establishing and implementing training programs on 
     use of noise abatement equipment; and
       ``(vi) implementing noise abatement plans;'';

       (III) by striking the undesignated matter following 
     paragraph (5); and
       (IV) by redesignating paragraphs (1) through (5) as 
     subparagraphs (A) through (E), respectively, and indenting 
     appropriately;

       (iii) by redesignating subsections (a) through (g) as 
     paragraphs (1) through (7), respectively, and indenting 
     appropriately;
       (iv) in the matter preceding paragraph (1) (as so 
     redesignated), by striking ``To promote'' and inserting the 
     following:
       ``(a) In General.--To promote''; and
       (v) by adding at the end the following:
       ``(b) Authorization of Appropriations.--There is authorized 
     to be appropriated to carry out this section $20,000,000 for 
     each of fiscal years 2027 through 2031, to remain available 
     until expended.''.
       (6) State and local capacity grants to address data center 
     environmental impacts.--
       (A) Definitions.--In this paragraph:
       (i) Cumulative impact.--The term ``cumulative impact'', 
     with respect to pollution impact, means the impact on the 
     environment resulting from the incremental impact of the 
     action when added to other past, present, and reasonably 
     foreseeable future actions.
       (ii) Eligible entity.--The term ``eligible entity'' means a 
     partnership between--

       (I) a State, Tribal, or local government; and
       (II) a community-based nonprofit organization.

       (iii) Program.--The term ``program'' means the program 
     established under subparagraph (B).
       (B) Establishment.--The Administrator shall establish a 
     program to award grants to eligible entities to carry out 
     activities that benefit disadvantaged communities, as 
     determined by the Administrator, in accordance with this 
     paragraph.
       (C) Grants.--
       (i) In general.--The Administrator shall award grants under 
     the program to eligible entities to carry out activities 
     described in clause (ii).
       (ii) Eligible activities.--An eligible entity receiving a 
     grant under the program may use funds from that grant for--

       (I) community-led evaluation, monitoring, prevention, and 
     remediation of air, water, and noise pollution impact, 
     including cumulative impacts, with an emphasis on projects 
     that prioritize evaluation, monitoring, prevention, and 
     remediation of cumulative impacts;
       (II) efforts to strengthen local enforcement and technical 
     capacity for inspectors, engineers, and permitting and 
     compliance staff with relevant State, Tribal, and local 
     environmental agencies; and
       (III) facilitating engagement of disadvantaged communities, 
     as determined by the Administrator, in State, Tribal, local, 
     and intergovernmental zoning, permitting, and regulatory 
     decisions, including through strategies that increase access 
     across languages, abilities, and media platforms.

       (iii) Term.--The term of a grant under the program shall be 
     up to 3 years.
       (D) Technical assistance.--The Administrator shall provide 
     technical assistance to eligible entities with respect to 
     grants awarded under the program.
       (E) Authorization of appropriations.--
       (i) In general.--There is authorized to be appropriated to 
     the Administrator for each

[[Page S5652]]

     of fiscal years 2027 through 2031, to remain available until 
     expended--

       (I) $560,000,000 to award grants under subparagraph (C); 
     and
       (II) $40,000,000 to provide technical assistance under 
     subparagraph (D).

       (ii) Administrative costs.--Of the amounts made available 
     under clause (i) in a fiscal year, the Administrator shall 
     reserve 7 percent to carry out this paragraph.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 6968. Mr. MARKEY submitted an amendment intended to be proposed to 
amendment SA 6883 proposed by Mr. Thune (for Mrs. Capito (for herself, 
Mr. Lee, Mr. Whitehouse, and Mr. Heinrich)) to the bill H.R. 2347, to 
amend the Internal Revenue Code of 1986 to exclude from gross income 
any damages, other than punitive damages, received on account of any 
sexual acts or sexual contact; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

        On page 345, between lines 2 and 3, insert the following:
       (3) Interagency consultation.--In developing the Data 
     Center Community Toolkit under paragraph (1), the Secretary 
     shall consult relevant Federal agencies in selecting and 
     verifying information and resources included.
       (4) Public comment.--In developing the Data Center 
     Community Toolkit under paragraph (1), the Secretary shall 
     solicit comments from the public to guide the types of 
     resources and information that should be included.

  SA 6969. Mr. MARKEY submitted an amendment intended to be proposed to 
amendment SA 6883 proposed by Mr. Thune (for Mrs. Capito (for herself, 
Mr. Lee, Mr. Whitehouse, and Mr. Heinrich)) to the bill H.R. 2347, to 
amend the Internal Revenue Code of 1986 to exclude from gross income 
any damages, other than punitive damages, received on account of any 
sexual acts or sexual contact; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

        On page 345, strike lines 4 through 21 and insert the 
     following:
       (1) In general.--At the request of a State, local, or 
     Tribal government, the Secretary shall--
       (A) request the applicable computational load site 
     developer to provide to the Secretary information, data, 
     analyses, models, assumptions, estimates, projections, and 
     other materials relating to the proposed computational load 
     site;
       (B) independently assess that information and those 
     materials, including by evaluating--
       (i) the reasonableness of the assumptions and methodologies 
     used;
       (ii) the reliability of the data;
       (iii) whether estimates and projections are adequately 
     supported by the information provided; and
       (iv) the technical reasonableness of the conclusions 
     reached; and
       (C) after receiving and assessing relevant information, the 
     Secretary shall--
       (i) transmit such information back to the applicable State, 
     local, or Tribal government; and
       (ii) make publicly available any information not considered 
     confidential.
                                 ______
                                 
  SA 6970. Mr. HEINRICH (for Mr. Wyden) submitted an amendment intended 
to be proposed to amendment SA 6883 proposed by Mr. Thune (for Mrs. 
Capito (for herself, Mr. Lee, Mr. Whitehouse, and Mr. Heinrich)) to the 
bill H.R. 2347, to amend the Internal Revenue Code of 1986 to exclude 
from gross income any damages, other than punitive damages, received on 
account of any sexual acts or sexual contact; which was ordered to lie 
on the table; as follows:

        At the appropriate place, insert the following:

     SEC. ____. PUBLICATION OF REPORTS ON INVESTIGATIONS RELATING 
                   TO EFFECTS ON NATIONAL SECURITY OF THE IMPORT 
                   OF CERTAIN ARTICLES.

       Not later than 30 days after the date of the enactment of 
     this Act, the Secretary of Commerce shall--
       (1) publish in the Federal Register--
       (A) the report on the findings of the investigation into 
     the effects of imports of polysilicon and its derivatives on 
     the national security of the United States that the Secretary 
     initiated on July 1, 2025, under section 232(b) of the Trade 
     Expansion Act of 1962 (19 U.S.C. 1862(b)), as required under 
     paragraph (3)(B) of that section; and
       (B) the report on the findings of the investigation into 
     the effects of imports of wind turbines and their parts and 
     components on the national security of the United States that 
     the Secretary initiated on August 13, 2025, under section 
     232(b) of the Trade Expansion Act of 1962 (19 U.S.C. 
     1862(b)), as required under paragraph (3)(B) of that section; 
     and
       (2) submit to Congress any portion of either such report 
     that contains classified information, which may be viewed 
     only by Members of Congress and their staff with appropriate 
     security clearances.

  SA 6971. Mr. HEINRICH (for Mr. Wyden (for himself, Mr. Merkley, and 
Ms. Cantwell)) submitted an amendment intended to be proposed to 
amendment SA 6883 proposed by Mr. Thune (for Mrs. Capito (for herself, 
Mr. Lee, Mr. Whitehouse, and Mr. Heinrich)) to the bill H.R. 2347, to 
amend the Internal Revenue Code of 1986 to exclude from gross income 
any damages, other than punitive damages, received on account of any 
sexual acts or sexual contact; which was ordered to lie on the table; 
as follows:

       At the end of title II of division B, add the following:

Subtitle H--Prohibition of Construction of Data Centers on Federal Land

     SEC. 2271. PROHIBITION OF CONSTRUCTION OF DATA CENTERS ON 
                   FEDERAL LAND.

       (a) Definitions.--In this section:
       (1) Data center.--The term ``data center'' has the meaning 
     given the term in section 453(a) of the Energy Independence 
     and Security Act of 2007 (42 U.S.C. 17112(a)).
       (2) Federal land.--
       (A) In general.--The term ``Federal land'' means--
       (i) National Forest System land; and
       (ii) land managed by the Secretary of the Interior.
       (B) Exclusions.--The term ``Federal land'' does not 
     include--
       (i) any land located within the boundaries of an Indian 
     reservation, pueblo, or rancheria; or
       (ii) any land not located within the boundaries of an 
     Indian reservation, pueblo, or rancheria, the title to which 
     is held--

       (I) in trust by the United States for the benefit of an 
     Indian Tribe or an individual Indian;
       (II) by an Indian Tribe or an individual Indian, subject to 
     restriction against alienation under laws of the United 
     States; or
       (III) by a dependent Indian community.

       (3) Indian tribe.--The term ``Indian Tribe'' has the 
     meaning given the term in section 4 of the Indian Self-
     Determination and Education Assistance Act (25 U.S.C. 5304).
       (4) National forest system.--The term ``National Forest 
     System'' has the meaning given the term in section 11(a) of 
     the Forest and Rangeland Renewable Resources Planning Act of 
     1974 (16 U.S.C. 1609(a)).
       (b) Prohibition on Construction of Data Centers.--
     Notwithstanding any other provision of law, the construction 
     of data centers on Federal land is prohibited.
       (c) Reversionary Clause Required for Federal Land 
     Conveyances.--Any conveyance of Federal land occurring on or 
     after the date of enactment of this Act shall include a 
     reversionary clause to ensure that the conveyed Federal land 
     shall revert to the United States if the conveyed Federal 
     land is used for a data center, unless the use of the 
     conveyed Federal land for a data center is specifically 
     authorized by an Act of Congress.
                                 ______

Mr. Thune (for Mrs. Capito (for herself, Mr. Lee, Mr. Whitehouse, and

        In section 1204, add at the end the following:
       (c) Compliance With Biological Opinion and Reinstatement of 
     Approval of Florida State Dredged and Fill Permitting 
     Program.--

[[Page S5653]]

       (1) Definitions.--In this subsection:
       (A) Agency action.--
       (i) In general.--The term ``Agency Action'' means the 
     approval by the Administrator of the Environmental Protection 
     Agency of the request of the State of Florida for the 
     assumption by the State of Florida of the administration and 
     permitting of a State permit program under section 404 of the 
     Federal Water Pollution Control Act (33 U.S.C. 1344), as 
     described in the notice of the Environmental Protection 
     Agency entitled ``EPA's Approval of Florida's Clean Water Act 
     Section 404 Assumption Request'' (85 Fed. Reg. 83553 
     (December 22, 2020)).
       (ii) Inclusion.--The term ``Agency Action'' includes other 
     activities.
       (B) Biological opinion.--
       (i) In general.--The term ``Biological Opinion'' means the 
     programmatic biological opinion issued by the United States 
     Fish and Wildlife Service entitled ``Programmatic Biological 
     Opinion for Environmental Protection Agency's Approval of 
     FDEP's Assumption of the Administration of the Dredge and 
     Fill Permitting Program under Section 404 of the Clean Water 
     Act'' (FWS Log Nos. 04E00000-2021-F-0001 and 04E00000-2021-B-
     0001) and dated November 17, 2020.
       (ii) Inclusion.--The term ``Biological Opinion'' includes 
     the incidental take statement described on pages 69 through 
     73 of the programmatic biological opinion described in clause 
     (i).
       (C) Other activities.--The term ``other activities'' has 
     the meaning given the term in the glossary of the Biological 
     Opinion.
       (2) Compliance with biological opinion.--For purposes of 
     the Agency Action--
       (A) the Biological Opinion is deemed to be compliant with, 
     and compliance with the Biological Opinion is deemed to be 
     compliance with, the requirements of the Endangered Species 
     Act of 1973 (16 U.S.C. 1531 et seq.); and
       (B) no additional consultation with the United States Fish 
     and Wildlife Service or the National Marine Fisheries Service 
     under section 7(a) of that Act (16 U.S.C. 1536(a)) is 
     required.
       (3) Reinstatement of agency action.--Notwithstanding any 
     other provision of law or any court order vacating or setting 
     aside the Agency Action, the Agency Action is reinstated and 
     shall have full force and effect beginning on the date of 
     enactment of this Act.

        Beginning on page 111, strike line 9 and all that follows

       ``(A) In general.--The scope of a certification provided 
     under this section shall be limited to ensuring, by a 
     preponderance of the evidence, compliance with water quality 
     requirements for any activity that may result in a discharge.
       ``(B) Prohibition.--Notwithstanding subparagraph (A), in 
     reviewing a certification application for a project described 
     in subsection (g)(2), no certifying authority may--
       ``(i) deny certification for any reason other than making a

     or

     and given the applicant a reasonable opportunity to provide 
     the additional materials or information described in that 
     communication, the application continues to lack the 
     information required under clause (ii) and, if applicable, 
     clause (iii) of that paragraph; or

  SA 6977. Mr. HEINRICH (for Mr. Welch) submitted an amendment intended 
to be proposed to amendment SA 6883 proposed by Mr. Thune (for Mrs. 
Capito (for herself, Mr. Lee, Mr. Whitehouse, and Mr. Heinrich)) to the 
bill H.R. 2347, to amend the Internal Revenue Code of 1986 to exclude 
from gross income any damages, other than punitive damages, received on 
account of any sexual acts or sexual contact; which was ordered to lie 
on the table; as follows:

        Beginning on page 125, strike line 2 and all that follows 
     through page 130, line 15, and insert the following:
       ``(1) Burdens of proof.--
       ``(A) In general.--When reviewing a denial of a 
     certification under this section, a court shall place the 
     burden of proof, as described in subparagraph (B), on the 
     certifying authority to demonstrate that no reasonable 
     modifications to or reasonable conditions on the 
     certification would make it possible to grant the 
     certification without violating water quality requirements.
       ``(B) Burdens of proof described.--The burden of proof 
     referred to in subparagraph (A) is--
       ``(i) in the case of a certification for a project 
     described in subsection (g)(2), a preponderance of the 
     evidence; and
       ``(ii) in the case of any other certification under this 
     section, clear and convincing evidence.
       ``(2) Conditions.--When reviewing a challenge to 1 or more 
     conditions required by a certifying authority under this 
     section for a project described in subsection (g)(2), a court 
     shall place the burden of proof on the certifying authority 
     to demonstrate, by clear and convincing evidence, that the 1 
     or more challenged conditions are--
       ``(A) necessary to allow the certification to be made 
     without violating water quality requirements; and
       ``(B) the least burdensome condition or set of conditions 
     for avoiding violation of water quality requirements.
       ``(g) Special Rule for Certain Certifications.--
       ``(1) Definition of interstate transmission line project.--
     In this subsection, the term `interstate transmission line 
     project' means a project for the construction, modification, 
     or upgrade of a transmission facility that is part of the 
     bulk-power system (as defined in section 215(a) of the 
     Federal Power Act (16 U.S.C. 824o(a))).
       ``(2) Limitation on certification requirements for certain 
     interstate linear infrastructure.--Notwithstanding the first 
     sentence of subsection (a)(1), any applicant for a 
     certificate of public convenience and necessity for a project 
     under section 7 of the Natural Gas Act (15 U.S.C. 717f) or a 
     Federal authorization or permit required to construct, 
     modify, or operate an interstate transmission line project 
     that may result in a discharge into the waters of the United 
     States shall provide to the Federal licensing or permitting 
     agency a certification from a certifying authority that such 
     discharge complies with water quality requirements or a 
     waiver pursuant to this section.
       ``(3) Certification of no limitation and standard.--In the 
     case of any discharge described in this subsection for which 
     there is not an applicable effluent limitation or other 
     limitation under sections 301(b) and 302 and for which there 
     is not an applicable standard under sections 306 and 307, the 
     certifying authority shall so certify, except that such a 
     certification shall not satisfy section 511(c).

[[Page S5654]]

       ``(4) Review for compliance.--Notwithstanding subsection 
     (a)(4)--
       ``(A) prior to the initial operation of any federally 
     licensed or permitted facility or activity that may result in 
     a discharge into the waters of the United States and with 
     respect to which a certification has been obtained pursuant 
     to this subsection, which facility or activity is not subject 
     to a Federal operating license or permit, the licensee or 
     permittee shall provide an opportunity for the certifying 
     authority to review the manner in which the facility or 
     activity shall be operated or conducted for the purposes of 
     assuring that applicable effluent limitations or other 
     limitations or other applicable water quality requirements 
     will not be violated;
       ``(B) on notification by the certifying authority that the 
     operation of any federally licensed or permitted facility or 
     activity described in subparagraph (A) will violate 
     applicable effluent limitations or other limitations or other 
     water quality requirements, the applicable Federal agency 
     may, after public hearing, suspend the Federal license or 
     permit; and
       ``(C) if a Federal license or permit is suspended pursuant 
     to subparagraph (B), the Federal license or permit shall 
     remain suspended until notification is received from the 
     certifying authority that there is reasonable assurance that 
     the facility or activity will not violate the applicable 
     provisions of sections 301, 302, 303, 306, and 307.
       ``(5) Limitation and monitoring requirements of certain 
     linear infrastructure certifications.--Notwithstanding 
     subsection (d)--
       ``(A) any certification provided under this subsection 
     shall set forth any effluent limitations and other 
     limitations and monitoring requirements necessary to assure 
     that any discharge subject to this subsection will comply 
     with the applicable provisions of sections 301, 302, 303, 
     306, and 307; and
       ``(B) any limitations and requirements set forth pursuant 
     to subparagraph (A) shall be imposed by the licensing or 
     permitting agency as a condition on any Federal license or 
     permit subject to the provisions of this section.
       ``(6) Reasonable period of time.--Notwithstanding the 
     periods of time in subsections (a)(1) and (e)(4), a licensing 
     or permitting agency shall, categorically or on a case-by-
     case basis for each certification application, establish a 
     reasonable period of time (not to exceed 9 months from the 
     date of receipt of the certification application) within 
     which a certifying authority shall issue a final action on 
     the certification application under this subsection, which 
     shall account for the time required under applicable State or 
     Tribal law for public notice and comment periods.
       ``(h) Definitions.--In this section:
       ``(1) Certification application.--The term `certification 
     application' means a request from an applicant for a 
     certification described in subsection (a).
       ``(2) Certifying authority.--The term `certifying 
     authority', with respect to a certification application, 
     means, as applicable--
       ``(A) the State or interstate agency to which the 
     certification application was submitted, including an Indian 
     Tribe that is treated as a State for purposes of this section 
     pursuant to section 131.8 of title 40, Code of Federal 
     Regulations; or
       ``(B) if no State or interstate agency has the authority to 
     provide the certification described in subsection (a), the 
     Administrator.
       ``(3) Water quality requirements.--
       ``(A) In general.--The term `water quality requirements' 
     means--
       ``(i) the applicable provisions of sections 301, 302, 303, 
     306, and 307;
       ``(ii) any Federal and State or Tribal laws (including 
     regulations) implementing sections 301, 302, 303, 306, and 
     307 that are applicable to discharges into waters of the 
     United States; and
       ``(iii) any other requirements under Federal and State or 
     Tribal laws (including regulations) that are applicable to 
     discharges into waters of the United States.
       ``(B) Exclusion.--The term `water quality requirements' 
     does not include any Federal and State or Tribal regulations 
     that solely relate to visual aesthetics that are applicable 
     to discharges into waters of the United States.''.

     SEC. 1303. PROJECTS IN THE OPERATIONAL RIGHT-OF-WAY.

       Section 7 of the Endangered Species Act of 1973 (16 U.S.C. 
     1536) is amended by adding at the end the following:
       ``(q) Projects in the Operational Right-of-way.--
     Notwithstanding subsections (g) and (h), any project that is 
     categorically excluded from the requirements of the National 
     Environmental Policy Act of 1969 (42 U.S.C. 4321 et seq.) 
     pursuant to section 1316 of the Moving Ahead for Progress in 
     the 21st Century Act (23 U.S.C. 109 note; Public Law 112-141) 
     shall be exempt from paragraphs (2), (3), and (4) of 
     subsection (a).''.

     SEC. 1304. STATE ASSUMPTION OF CONSULTATION RESPONSIBILITIES.

       The Endangered Species Act of 1973 is amended by inserting 
     after section 7 (16 U.S.C. 1536) the following:

     ``SEC. 7A. STATE ASSUMPTION OF CONSULTATION RESPONSIBILITIES.

       ``(a) Definitions.--In this section:
       ``(1) Program.--The term `program' means the program 
     carried out by the Secretary pursuant to this section.
       ``(2) Secretary.--The term `Secretary' means the Secretary 
     of the Interior.
       ``(3) State agency.--The term `State agency' means any 
     agency, department, board, commission, or other governmental 
     entity in a State with primary jurisdiction over 
     environmental or wildlife management matters in the State, as 
     determined by the Governor of the State.
       ``(b) Program.--
       ``(1) In general.--Except as provided in this subsection, 
     the Secretary shall carry

[[Page S5655]]

     out a program under which the Secretary may assign the 
     consultation responsibilities of the Secretary described in 
     paragraph (4)(A) to States.
       ``(2) Limitations on assumptions.--
       ``(A) In general.--Except as provided in subparagraph (B), 
     the Secretary may not assign the consultation 
     responsibilities of the Secretary described in paragraph 
     (4)(A) to any State for a project or agency action (as 
     referred to in section 7) that exceeds the boundaries of the 
     State.
       ``(B) Limitation.--For any project or agency action (as 
     referred to in section 7) that is solely within the 
     boundaries of 1 State, but that is within an area affected 
     that exceeds the boundaries of the State, a State that 
     assumes responsibility under paragraph (4) for the project or 
     agency action (as referred to in section 7) shall consult 
     with--
       ``(i) if any other State has assumed responsibility under 
     that paragraph, the jurisdiction of which is within that area 
     affected, such State; or
       ``(ii) if there is no State described in clause (i), the 
     Secretary.
       ``(3) Implementing regulation.--
       ``(A) In general.--Not later than 270 days after the date 
     of enactment of the Bipartisan American Affordability and 
     Jobs Act of 2026, the Secretary shall issue a proposed rule 
     to establish a program that would comply with the 
     requirements of this section and ensure that approved State 
     assumption programs adequately carry out the consultation 
     requirements under section 7 (referred to in this section as 
     the `implementing regulation').
       ``(B) Public comment.--The proposed rule issued under 
     subparagraph (A) shall be made available for public comment 
     for a period of not less than 60 days, during which the 
     Secretary shall solicit comment from States on the proposed 
     rule.
       ``(C) Limitation.--The rule issued under subparagraph (A) 
     shall not include any requirement that is not necessary to 
     carry out this section.
       ``(4) Assumption of responsibility.--
       ``(A) In general.--Subject to the requirements of this 
     section and the implementing regulation, with the written 
     agreement of the Secretary and a State, which may be in the 
     form of a memorandum of understanding, the Secretary may 
     assign, and the State may assume, the consultation 
     responsibilities of the Secretary within the boundary of the 
     State under subsections (a), (b), and (c) of section 7.
       ``(B) Certain projects and agency actions.--A State may not 
     assume responsibility under the program until the date on 
     which--
       ``(i) the implementing regulation takes effect; and
       ``(ii) the State certifies to the Secretary that the State 
     has secured the financial, legal, personnel, and other 
     resources described in subsection (c)(2)(B).
       ``(C) Federal responsibility.--Any responsibility of the 
     Secretary not explicitly assumed by the State by written 
     agreement under the program shall remain the responsibility 
     of the Secretary.
       ``(c) State Participation.--
       ``(1) Requirements for participating states.--Each State is 
     eligible to participate in the program if the State meets 
     each requirement described in this section and the 
     implementing regulation.
       ``(2) Application.--
       ``(A) In general.--A State seeking to participate in the 
     program shall submit to the Secretary an application at such 
     time, in such manner, and containing such information as the 
     Secretary may require, in accordance with subparagraph (B).
       ``(B) Requirements.--As part of the implementing 
     regulation, the Secretary shall establish requirements 
     relating to applications submitted by States under 
     subparagraph (A), including, at a minimum, requirements that 
     a State provide--
       ``(i) verification of the financial and legal resources 
     necessary to carry out the authority that may be granted to 
     the State under the program;
       ``(ii) verification of the personnel resources or a plan to 
     hire the personnel, not later than 1 year after the date on 
     which the application is approved, necessary to carry out the 
     authority that may be granted to the State under the program; 
     and
       ``(iii) evidence of the notice and solicitation of public 
     comment by the State relating to participation of the State 
     in the program, including--

       ``(5) Preliminary injunctions.--
       ``(A) In general.--Subject to subparagraph (B), a court may 
     issue a preliminary injunction for an authorization that does 
     not comply with the requirements of this Act when--
       ``(i) there is a substantial likelihood the authorization 
     violates a Federal law other than--

       ``(I) this Act; or
       ``(II) division A of subtitle III of title 54, United 
     States Code;

       ``(ii) necessary to prevent irreparable harm to the 
     plaintiff;
       ``(iii) the balance of equities favors the plaintiff; and
       ``(iv) a preliminary injunction is in the public interest.
       ``(B) Requirements.--A preliminary injunction issued by a 
     court under subparagraph (A) shall last no longer than the 
     time the court finds is reasonably necessary--
       ``(i) for the correction of the violation described in 
     subparagraph (A)(i); and
       ``(ii) to prevent the irreparable harm described in 
     subparagraph (A)(ii).
       ``(6) Treatment of supplemental or revised environmental 
     documents.--
       ``(A) In general.--For purposes of a claim--
       ``(i) a supplemental or revised environmental document for 
     an authorization, when required by court order, shall be 
     considered to be a separate environmental document; and
       ``(ii) the deadline for filing a claim for judicial review 
     of an authorization described in clause (i) shall be the date 
     that is 150 days after the date on which the authorization 
     for which the supplemental environmental document is prepared 
     is made public by the agency, unless a shorter time is 
     specified in the Federal law pursuant to which judicial 
     review is authorized.
       ``(B) Limitation.--A claim for review of an authorization 
     described in subparagraph (A)(i) shall be limited to 
     information contained in the final supplemental environmental 
     documents that was not contained in a previous environmental 
     document for the authorization.
       ``(7) Actions for use of tribal trust resources.--

       ``(1) Effect of certain certification actions.--
       ``(A) Certification.--If a certifying authority grants a 
     certification application, the agency with jurisdiction over 
     the Federal license or permit for which the certification 
     application was made shall consider water quality 
     requirements satisfied for the purpose of considering the 
     application for the Federal license or permit.
       ``(B) Certification with conditions.--
       ``(i) In general.--If a certifying authority grants a 
     certification application with conditions, the agency with 
     jurisdiction over the Federal license or permit for which the 
     certification application was made shall consider water 
     quality requirements satisfied for the purpose of considering 
     the application for the Federal license or permit, subject to 
     the conditions included in the certification.
       ``(ii) Savings clause.--Nothing in this subparagraph 
     affects paragraph (2)(B).
       ``(C) Waiver of certification.--If a certifying authority 
     waives the certification requirement for a certification 
     application, the Federal licensing or permitting agency for 
     which the certification application was made shall review the 
     certification application for compliance with water quality 
     requirements.

  SA 6995. Ms. CANTWELL (for herself and Mr. Wyden) submitted an 
amendment intended to be proposed to amendment SA 6883 proposed by Mr. 
Thune (for Mrs. Capito (for herself, Mr. Lee, Mr. Whitehouse, and Mr.

       In section 1401, add at the end the following:
       (d) Exclusion of Certain Federal Land in Alaska.--
       (1) Definitions.--In this subsection:
       (A) Arctic national wildlife refuge.--
       (i) In general.--The term ``Arctic National Wildlife 
     Refuge'' means the Arctic National Wildlife Refuge designated 
     under section 303(2) of the Alaska National Interest Lands 
     Conservation Act (Public Law 96-487; 94 Stat. 2390).
       (ii) Inclusion.--The term ``Arctic National Wildlife 
     Refuge'' includes the Coastal Plain (as defined in section 
     20001(a) of Public Law 115-97 (16 U.S.C. 3143 note)).
       (B) Covered activity.--The term ``covered activity'' means 
     any project, action, or activity, including any lease sale, 
     lease, exploration, seismic survey, road, pipeline, or right-
     of-way, that is located wholly or partially on Federal land 
     within--
       (i) the Arctic National Wildlife Refuge; or
       (ii) the National Petroleum Reserve in Alaska.
       (C) National petroleum reserve in alaska.--The term 
     ``National Petroleum Reserve in Alaska'' means the National 
     Petroleum Reserve in Alaska designated under section 102 of 
     the Naval Petroleum Reserves Production Act of 1976 (42 
     U.S.C. 6502).
       (2) Exclusion.--Subsection (b) shall not apply to--
       (A) any Federal authorization or permit, lease, or other 
     Federal approval for a covered activity; or
       (B) any action by a Federal department or agency with 
     respect to a covered activity.
       (3) Savings clause.--Nothing in this subsection shall limit 
     the authority of any Federal department or agency under any 
     other provision of law with respect to a covered activity.

  SA 6996. Ms. DUCKWORTH (for herself and Mr. Grassley) submitted an 
amendment intended to be proposed to amendment SA 6883 proposed by Mr.

Heinrich)) to the bill H.R. 2347, to amend the Internal Revenue Code of 
1986 to exclude from gross income any damages, other than punitive 
damages, received on account of any sexual acts or sexual contact; 
which was ordered to lie on the table; as follows:

       Starting on page 238 of the amendment, line 7, strike all 
     that follows through page 269, line 9, and insert the 
     following:
       (1) in the matter preceding paragraph (1), by striking 
     ``Except'' and all that follows through ``finds that'' and 
     inserting ``Except as provided in subsections (d)(1) and (i), 
     the Commission shall, after notice and an opportunity for 
     hearing, including a public comment period of at least 90 
     days, issue one or more permits to individual or joint 
     applicants for the construction or modification of electric 
     transmission facilities necessary in the national interest if 
     the Commission finds that'';
       (2) in paragraph (1)--
       (A) in subparagraph (A)(i), by inserting ``or 
     modification'' after ``siting''; and
       (B) in subparagraph (C)--
       (i) in the matter preceding clause (i), by inserting ``or 
     modification'' after ``siting''; and
       (ii) in clause (i), by striking ``the later of'' in the 
     matter preceding subclause (I) and all that follows through 
     the semicolon at the end of subclause (II) and inserting 
     ``the date on which the application was filed with the State 
     commission or other entity;''; and
       (3) by striking paragraphs (2) through (6) and inserting 
     the following:
       ``(2) the proposed facilities will be used for the 
     transmission of electric energy in interstate or foreign 
     commerce;
       ``(3) the proposed construction or modification is 
     consistent with the public interest;
       ``(4) the proposed construction or modification will 
     protect or benefit consumers, provide improved reliability, 
     and facilitate just and reasonable rates;
       ``(5) the electric transmission facilities--
       ``(A) are--
       ``(i) capable of transmitting electric energy at a voltage 
     of not less than 230 kilovolts; or
       ``(ii) necessary to safely and reliably interconnect 1 or 
     more facilities described in clause (i) for which the permit 
     is provided; or
       ``(B) in the case of facilities that include advanced 
     transmission conductors (including superconductors), as 
     defined by the Commission, are capable of transmitting 
     electric energy at voltages determined to be appropriate by 
     the Commission for facilities of a comparable throughput 
     specifically with respect to an application for an individual 
     project under this section;
       ``(6) if applicable, the proposed modification (including 
     reconductoring) will maximize, to the extent reasonable and 
     economical, the transmission capabilities of existing towers, 
     structures, or rights-of-way; and
       ``(7) if applicable, and to the maximum extent practicable 
     and reasonable, the proposed construction will be sited--
       ``(A) near and parallel to roads, the rights-of-way of 
     railways, or along division lines of lands, according to a 
     government survey;
       ``(B) so as not to interfere with the use by the public of 
     highways or streams; and
       ``(C) so as not to unnecessarily interfere with the use of 
     any land by the affected landowner.''.
       (c) State Siting and Consultation.--Section 216 of the 
     Federal Power Act (16 U.S.C. 824p) is amended by striking 
     subsection (d) and inserting the following:
       ``(d) State Siting and Consultation.--
       ``(1) Preservation of state siting authority.--The 
     Commission shall have no authority to issue a permit under 
     subsection (b) for the construction or modification of an 
     electric transmission facility within a State except as 
     provided in paragraph (1) of that subsection.
       ``(2) Consultation.--In any proceeding before the 
     Commission under subsection (b), the Commission shall afford 
     each State in which a transmission facility covered by the 
     permit is or will be located, each affected Federal agency 
     and Indian Tribe, private property owners, and other 
     interested persons, a reasonable opportunity to present their 
     views and recommendations with respect to the need for and 
     impact of a facility covered by the permit.
       ``(3) Landowner input.--In authorizing the construction or 
     modification of an electric transmission facility under 
     subsection (b), the Commission shall take into account 
     landowner input.
       ``(4) State record.--In any proceeding before the 
     Commission under subsection (b), each State in which a 
     transmission facility covered by the permit is or will be 
     located may file with the Commission, and the Commission 
     shall consider, any part of the State record, if applicable, 
     that the State finds to be relevant to the determination of 
     the Commission.
       ``(5) Pre-filing processes.--Any Commission-related pre-
     filing processes relating to an application submitted under 
     subsection (c) may commence before, but shall commence no 
     later than, an application is submitted to the applicable 
     State commission or other entity that has authority to 
     approve the siting of the applicable facility.''.
       (d) Rights-of-Way.--Section 216(e) of the Federal Power Act 
     (16 U.S.C. 824p(e)) is amended by striking ``or a State'' in 
     paragraph (1) and by striking ``shall conform as nearly as 
     practicable to the practice and procedure in a similar action 
     or proceeding in the courts of the State in which the 
     property is located'' in paragraph (3) and inserting ``shall 
     be in accordance with rule 71.1 of the Federal Rules of Civil 
     Procedure.''.
       (e) Cost Allocation.--
       (1) In general.--Section 216 of the Federal Power Act (16 
     U.S.C. 824p) is amended by striking subsection (f) and 
     inserting the following:
       ``(f) Cost Allocation.--
       ``(1) Transmission tariffs.--For the purposes of this 
     section and except as provided in paragraph (2), any 
     transmitting utility that owns, controls, or operates, or 
     proposes to construct, electric transmission facilities with 
     respect to which the Commission finds that the criteria set 
     forth in paragraphs (2) through (5) and, if applicable, (6) 
     of subsection (b) are satisfied, and any entity that will 
     become a transmitting utility on completion of 1 or more 
     proposed electric transmission facilities with respect to 
     which the Commission finds that those criteria are satisfied, 
     shall file a tariff or tariff revision with the Commission 
     pursuant to section 205 and the regulations of the Commission 
     allocating the costs of those new or modified electric 
     transmission facilities. The issuance of a permit under 
     subsection (b) shall not be required to make, or otherwise 
     act as a prerequisite to making, that filing pursuant to 
     section 205, provided that the Commission finds that the 
     criteria set forth in paragraphs (2) through (5) and, as 
     applicable, (6) or (7) of subsection (b) are satisfied with 
     respect to the applicable facilities.
       ``(2) Exception.--In the case of an exempt transmitting 
     utility (as defined in section 225(a)) that owns, controls, 
     or operates electric transmission facilities described in 
     paragraph (1), the Commission, notwithstanding section 
     211A(c), shall require the exempt transmitting utility to 
     provide transmission

[[Page S5658]]

     services over such facilities pursuant to section 211A and 
     the regulations of the Commission allocating the costs of the 
     new or modified transmission facilities.
       ``(3) Transmission benefits.--The Commission shall require 
     that tariffs or tariff revisions filed under this section are 
     just and reasonable and allocate the costs of providing 
     service to customers that benefit, in accordance with the 
     cost-causation principle and at least roughly commensurate 
     with the benefits, including through--
       ``(A) improved reliability;
       ``(B) reduced congestion;
       ``(C) reduced power losses;
       ``(D) greater carrying capacity that delivers improved 
     reliability or reductions in the cost of delivered power;
       ``(E) reduced operating reserve requirements; and
       ``(F) improved access to generation that achieves 
     reductions in the cost of delivered power.
       ``(4) Ratepayer protection.--Customers that receive no 
     benefit, or benefits that are trivial in relation to the 
     costs sought to be allocated, from existing or proposed 
     electric transmission facilities the costs of which are 
     allocated pursuant to paragraph (1) shall not be 
     involuntarily allocated any of the costs of those 
     transmission facilities, provided, however, that nothing in 
     this section shall prevent a transmitting utility from 
     recovering all or part of such costs through voluntary 
     agreement with its customers and allocating any remaining 
     costs in accordance with this subsection.''.
       (2) Savings provision.--If the Federal Energy Regulatory 
     Commission finds that the considerations under paragraphs (2) 
     through (5) and, as applicable, (6) or (7) of subsection (b) 
     of section 216 of the Federal Power Act (16 U.S.C. 824p) (as 
     amended by subsection (b)) are satisfied, nothing in this 
     section or the amendments made by this section shall be 
     construed to exclude transmission facilities located on the 
     outer Continental Shelf from being eligible for cost 
     allocation established under subsection (f)(1) of that 
     section (as amended by paragraph (1)).
       (f) Coordination of Federal Authorizations for Transmission 
     Facilities.--Section 216(h) of the Federal Power Act (16 
     U.S.C. 824p(h)) is amended--
       (1) in paragraph (1), by adding at the end the following:
       ``(C) The term `Federal authorization' includes a 
     certification under section 401 of the Federal Water 
     Pollution Control Act (33 U.S.C. 1341) from a certifying 
     authority (as defined in subsection (h) of that section) and 
     any other permit, license, concurrence, or approval of a 
     State administrative agency required under Federal law, other 
     than the Coastal Zone Management Act of 1972 (16 U.S.C. 1451 
     et seq.), in order to site a transmission facility.'';
       (2) in paragraph (2), by striking the period at the end and 
     inserting the following: ``, except that--
       ``(A) the Commission shall act as the lead agency in the 
     case of facilities permitted under subsection (b) or section 
     225; and
       ``(B) the Department of the Interior shall act as the lead 
     agency in the case of facilities located on a lease, 
     easement, or right-of-way granted by the Secretary of the 
     Interior under section 8(p)(1)(C) of the Outer Continental 
     Shelf Lands Act (43 U.S.C. 1337(p)(1)(C)).'';
       (3) in each of paragraphs (3), (4)(B), (4)(C), (5)(B), 
     (6)(A), (7)(A), (7)(B)(i), (8)(A)(i), and (9), by striking 
     ``Secretary'' each place it appears and inserting ``lead 
     agency'';
       (4) in paragraph (4)(A), by striking ``As head of the lead 
     agency, the Secretary'' and inserting ``The lead agency'';
       (5) in paragraph (5)(A), by striking ``As lead agency head, 
     the Secretary'' and inserting ``The lead agency'';
       (6) in paragraph (7)--
       (A) in subparagraph (A), by striking ``18 months after the 
     date of enactment of this section'' and inserting ``18 months 
     after the date of enactment of the Bipartisan American 
     Affordability and Jobs Act of 2026''; and
       (B) in subparagraph (B)(i), by striking ``1 year after the 
     date of enactment of this section'' and inserting ``18 months 
     after the date of enactment of the Bipartisan American 
     Affordability and Jobs Act of 2026''; and
       (7) by adding at the end the following:
       ``(10) Any order or action of a Federal agency or State 
     administrative agency acting pursuant to Federal law to 
     issue, condition, or deny a Federal authorization shall be 
     subject to judicial review in the same courts in which an 
     order of the Commission is subject to judicial review under
